{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that the stationary spin autocorrelation of the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at inverse temperature <i>β</i> = 1 has a common functional scaling limit for Gaussian and Rademacher couplings. With rate-one heat-bath clocks at every site, time is scaled by <i>n</i><sup>2/3</sup> and the mean spin autocorrelation is multiplied by <i>n</i><sup>1/3</sup>. The functions converge in law uniformly on compact positive-time intervals. Their limiting law is not a point mass: it retains sample-to-sample randomness, and its functions decay to zero at large times.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the stationary spin autocorrelation of the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at inverse temperature <i>β</i> = 1 has a common functional scaling limit for Gaussian and Rademacher couplings. With rate-one heat-bath clocks at every site, time is scaled by <i>n</i><sup>2/3</sup> and the mean spin autocorrelation is multiplied by <i>n</i><sup>1/3</sup>. The functions converge in law uniformly on compact positive-time intervals. Their limiting law is not a point mass: it retains sample-to-sample randomness, and its functions decay to zero at large times.

- **claim-functional-universality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a Borel probability law $Q_{\mathrm{crit}}$ on $C_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,\infty))$ such that, for each of the two choices of $D$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}_D(A_{n,J})\Longrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{crit}} \qquad(n\to\infty).$$ The law $Q_{\mathrm{crit}}$ is not a point mass. It assigns probability one to nonzero, nonnegative, nonincreasing functions $f$ satisfying $\lim_{s\to\infty}f(s)=0$. The Gaussian and Rademacher models use exactly the normalization in <a href="#eq:intro-rescaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-rescaling">[eq:intro-rescaling]</a>.
  - Explanation: The stationary critical autocorrelation has the same random functional limit for Gaussian and Rademacher couplings. The limit retains disorder randomness and almost surely decays at large times.
- **claim-goe-spectral-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-spectral)): The GOE eigenvalues have the following additional properties.* 1. *$\max_a\|\lambda_a\|\le3$ with probability tending to one.* 2. *There are tight random bounds $K_n\ge0$ and $C_n>0$ such that $g_{a,n}\ge C_n^{-1}a^{2/3}$ for every $a>K_n$.* 3. *For every $\epsilon>0$, $$\label{eq:edge-trace-double-limit} \lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb P\!\left( g_{1,n}+L\le0\ \text{or}\ \left\|n^{1/3}-\sum_{a=1}^n\frac1{g_{a,n}+L}-\sqrt L\right\| >\epsilon\right)=0.$$* *In the event appearing in the last formula, the sum is evaluated only when $g_{1,n}+L>0$.
  - Explanation: The spectrum has a bounded norm with high probability, increasing edge gaps beyond a tight index, and a renormalized resolvent trace limit.
- **claim-conditioned-edge-equilibrium** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-edge-conditioned-law)): Formula <a href="#eq:source-1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-1">[eq:source-1]</a> defines a finite random variable, and $$D_n(b_n):=n^{1/3}-\sum_{a=1}^n l_{a,n} \ \Longrightarrow\ D(b)$$ jointly with the edge coordinates. Almost surely $\sum_a l_a=\infty$. The law of $(Z_a)_{a\ge1}$ conditioned on $S=D(b)$ has a version $\Pi=\Pi_g$ characterized by continuous conditional densities. It is independent of the admissible choice of $b$.* *Let $\mu^{\rm sp}$ denote normalized surface measure on the sphere of radius $\sqrt n$, tilted by $\exp(X^{\mathsf T}WX/2)$. The fixed-coordinate marginals of $n^{-1/3}U^{\mathsf T}X$ under $\mu^{\rm sp}$ converge jointly in distribution with the eigenvalues to the random marginals of $\Pi$. On the spectral representations of Remark <a href="#rem:edge-tight-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rem:edge-tight-convergence">3</a>, this is ordinary weak convergence of measures, including convergence of every fixed polynomial moment. On the sets in Remark <a href="#rem:edge-tight-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rem:edge-tight-convergence">3</a>, for every fixed $0\le j<\infty$ one has $$\label{eq:edge-coordinate-moments} \int \|x_a\|^j\,d\Pi\le C_j l_a^{j/2}, \qquad \mu^{\rm sp}\|x_a\|^j\le C_j l_{a,n}^{j/2},$$ uniformly in the retained coordinate $a$.* *For any sufficiently large fixed $m$, the tail $(x_a)_{a>m}$ under $\Pi$ has a density relative to $\bigotimes_{a>m}N(0,l_a)$. Conditional on this tail, the first $m$ coordinates lie on the sphere of squared radius $$\label{eq:edge-polar-radius} D(b)+\sum_{a\le m}l_a-\sum_{a>m}(x_a^2-l_a),$$ with the Gaussian angular density on that sphere. In particular, $\sum_a(x_a^2-l_a)=D(b)$ holds $\Pi$-almost surely.
  - Explanation: The renormalized constraint defines a conditioned Gaussian edge measure independent of the auxiliary admissible parameter. Spherical edge marginals and their fixed polynomial moments converge to this measure.
- **claim-spherical-bulk-weight** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-bulk-weight)): For a spherical Gibbs spin, the random probability measure $$\frac1n\sum_a Y_a^2\delta_{\lambda_a}$$ converges in probability, conditionally on the spectral data as above, to $(2-\lambda)^{-1}d\rho_{\rm sc}(\lambda)$.
  - Explanation: The spherical spin distributes its bulk spectral mass according to the semicircle measure weighted by the inverse distance from the upper edge.
- **claim-haar-biased-spherical-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-biased-law)): Under $\mathbf P$, $U^{\mathsf T}X$ has exactly the spherical Gibbs law in spectral coordinates. After the row sign change sending $X$ to $\mathbf1$, conditional on $y=U^{\mathsf T}X$ the eigenframe is Haar subject to $Uy=\mathbf1$. Moreover, on the eigenvalue events used below, $$\label{eq:edge-uncapped-comparison} E_U(L_\circ-1)^2=o(1).$$
  - Explanation: Partition-function bias makes the rotated cube spin exactly spherical. The remaining eigenframe has a conditional Haar law, and the bias approaches the ordinary frame law in the stated regime.
- **claim-gaussian-cap-inputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-edge-cap-inputs)): Fix a finite field radius $R$ and positive fixed error tolerances. There are sufficiently small constants $p_{\rm top},s_0>0$ such that the following hold with probability tending to one in the range $np^3\ge c\sqrt{\log n}$ and $p\le p_{\rm top}$.* **Spectral and spherical estimates, at every scale.* The spectral dimension satisfies $\dim\mathcal H_p\asymp k_p$, $d_1\ge-o(p^2)$ uniformly in the cap range, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}R_{p,0}^2=O(n/p)$. On $\|\alpha\|\le s_0p^2$, $\ell_p(\alpha)\asymp p^2M_p$ and $\ell_p'(\alpha)\asymp M_p$. For $\\|h\\|\le Rp^2M_p$, the saddle precision has gap comparable to $p^2$. At $\alpha=\alpha_p(h)$, the density at $n$ of the squared norm of $\mathsf N(R_{p,\alpha}h,R_{p,\alpha})$ is comparable to $\sqrt{p/n}$. After multiplication by $e^{v^{\mathsf T}X}$ and renormalization, the Gaussian mean becomes $R_{p,\alpha}(h+v)$ while its covariance remains $R_{p,\alpha}$; its squared-norm density at $n$ is at most a fixed multiple of $\sqrt{p/n}$. For $m$ replicas, where $m$ is fixed, consider rowwise linear or quadratic tilts that preserve Gaussianity. The joint density of their squared norms is at most $C_m(p/n)^{m/2}$ under the following hypotheses. The spectral rows remain independent after the rowwise tilt, the tilted precision is positive definite, and on at least $ck_p$ rows the post-tilt row covariance $C_a$ satisfies $c p^{-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}_m\preceq C_a\preceq C p^{-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}_m$. All comparison constants here are fixed.* **Curvature estimates, at every scale.* On the annulus, for any prescribed fixed $\epsilon_0>0$, $$\label{eq:source-6} K_p\preceq(1+\epsilon_0)p^{-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathcal H_p},\qquad p^2(K_p)_{ee}\le1-\epsilon_1, \quad e=h/\\|h\\|,\quad \epsilon_1=0.1.$$ On the entire field ball one also has $K_p\preceq C_Rp^{-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathcal H_p}$, where $C_R$ may be chosen before $\epsilon_0$.* **Static comparisons and cube projection tails, on geometric lists.* For any prescribed fixed geometric list of scales, uniformly over the field ball, $$\|\phi_p(h)-\phi_p^{\rm sp}(h)\|\le\epsilon_v k_p, \qquad \\|P_pm_p(h)-R_{p,\alpha_p(h)}h\\|\le\epsilon_gM_p.$$ Any fixed finite family of such lists is allowed. For each $h\in\mathcal H_p$ with $\\|h\\|\le Rp^2M_p$, the cube posterior $\mu_{p,h}$ satisfies, uniformly for every $v\ge v_0$ with a sufficiently large fixed threshold $v_0$, $$\mu_{p,h}\{\\|P_pX\\|>vM_p\}\le C\exp(-ak_pv^6).$$ For $v>p^{-1/2}$ the event is empty, since $\\|X\\|=\sqrt n$. Here $a>0$, and the same bound holds under the uncapped zero-field cube measure $\mu$ at the same list scales.* *The spherical projection-tail estimate holds at every scale in the stated range, for capped bounded-field and uncapped zero-field measures. Also, at every such cap scale, for independent spherical replicas at a common field in the prescribed ball, $$(\mu_{p,h}^{\rm sp})^{\otimes2} \{\|n^{-1}X^{\mathsf T}X'\|>\rho\} \le C\exp(-cn\rho^3),\qquad Cp\le\rho\le\rho_0,$$ for a sufficiently small fixed $\rho_0>0$. A fixed finite number of replicas obeys the corresponding bound for any pair.
  - Explanation: The Gaussian cap inputs control spectral dimension, spherical saddles, norm densities, posterior curvature, projection tails, and replica overlaps. Static cube comparisons initially apply on prescribed geometric scale lists.
- **claim-cap-scale-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-static-interpolation)): The value, projected-mean, and cube projection-tail conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:edge-cap-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:edge-cap-inputs">7</a> hold at every scale in the stated range, after prescribing slightly smaller errors on a sufficiently fine fixed geometric list and decreasing the upper cutoff.
  - Explanation: Projection tails and control of nearby cap weights extend the static comparison from geometric lists to all intermediate scales.
- **claim-observation-path-annulus** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-observations-annulus)): There are $a,\xi>0$ and a sufficiently small fixed upper scale $p_{\mathrm{top}}>0$ such that $$\label{eq:source-7} \|\alpha_p(h_p)\|\le C k_p^{-a}p^2 \qquad(r\le p\le p_{\mathrm{top}})$$ outside a set of $\mathbf P$-probability at most $C\exp(-n^\xi)$. On disorder events of probability arbitrarily close to one, the same whole-path statement holds under $Q_\mu$, with a possibly smaller $\xi$.
  - Explanation: The observation path remains in a shrinking saddle annulus except on a small path event. The result transfers to ordinary quenched observation laws on disorder sets of arbitrarily high probability.
- **claim-replica-likelihood-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-observations-replicas)): Fix a bounded range for $\\|h\\|/(p^2M_p)$ and take the upper scale sufficiently small. Uniformly for fields in this range, $$\label{eq:source-9} E_U L_p(h)^2\le \exp(C+Cnp^4).$$ For a sufficiently large fixed $C_1$, the contribution to this normalized second moment of cube pairs satisfying $\|n^{-1}X\cdot X'\|>C_1p$ is at most $C\exp(-a k_p)$. At $p=r$, the stronger estimates $$\label{eq:source-10} \begin{split} E_U(L_r-1)^2&\le Cn^{-1/4},\\ E_U\!\left[L_r^2\\|m_r-m_r^{\mathrm{sp}}\\|^2\right] &\le C M_r^2 n^{-1/4} \end{split}$$ hold for the full mean vectors, written in spectral coordinates. The first bound also holds for the uncapped zero-field likelihood $L_\circ$. The pair-tail assertion at scale $r$ also holds in that uncapped case.
  - Explanation: Replica overlap comparison bounds the likelihood second moment. At the smallest scale it also controls the discrepancy of the full posterior mean vectors.
- **claim-cube-edge-marginal-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-observations-static)): Jointly with the spectral variables, in the sense of Remark <a href="#rem:edge-tight-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rem:edge-tight-convergence">3</a>, the quenched cube integrals of continuous cylinder functions of polynomial growth converge to their integrals under $\Pi$. In particular, all fixed moments of any fixed finite set of scaled edge coordinates converge.
  - Explanation: Quenched cube cylinder integrals converge to the conditioned edge measure, including all fixed moments of a fixed finite coordinate set.
- **claim-mean-moments-initial-score** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-observations-means)): For every fixed $j<\infty$, after reducing the fixed upper scale if needed, $$\label{eq:source-11} \mathbf E\bigl(\\|m_p\\|/M_p\bigr)^j +\mathbf E\bigl(\\|m_p^{\mathrm{sp}}\\|/M_p\bigr)^j\le C_j.$$ At the smallest scale, $$\label{eq:source-12} \mathbf E\frac{\\|m_r-m_r^{\mathrm{sp}}\\|^2}{M_r^2} \le Cn^{-1/20},\qquad \mathbf E\\|\nabla_{u_r}\log L_r\\|^4=o(1).$$
  - Explanation: Posterior means have bounded normalized moments. The smallest-scale mean discrepancy decays, and the likelihood score has vanishing fourth moment.
- **claim-integrated-observation-score** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-observations-score)): There is a fixed $\tau>0$ such that, on each scale interval of a fixed bounded ratio about $q$, $$\label{eq:source-13} \int\\|a_p^{\mathrm{err}}\\|^2\,d(p^2) \le Ck_q(q^\tau+k_q^{-\tau})$$ outside a set of $\mathbf P$-probability at most $C\exp(-n^\xi)$. The bound holds simultaneously on a geometric list of intervals, with fixed enlargements, and also holds in expectation.
  - Explanation: Likelihood dissipation controls the accumulated posterior mean error on scale windows, simultaneously over a geometric list and also in expectation.
- **claim-integrated-covariance-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-observations-curvature)): Put $D_p^K=p^2K_p-\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$ on $\mathcal H_p$. After decreasing $\tau>0$ if necessary, $$\label{eq:source-14} \int_q^{2q}\frac{\\|D_p^K\\|_{\mathrm F}^2}{k_p}\,d\log p \le C(q^\tau+k_q^{-\tau})$$ outside an event whose intersection with $\mathcal G_n$ has $\mathbf P$-probability at most $C\exp(-n^\xi)$. Fixed enlargements of the interval are allowed, and the assertions hold simultaneously on a geometric list.
  - Explanation: The normalized covariance error is small after logarithmic scale integration. Its exceptional event is measured before restriction to the common good-disorder event.
- **claim-angular-observation-score** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-observations-angular)): For a cap field $h$, write $\widetilde h=U^{\mathsf T}h$ and $\widetilde\phi_p(\widetilde h)=\phi_p(U\widetilde h)$. Let $A$ be a fixed skew-symmetric matrix supported on finitely many leading spectral coordinates, and set $\mathcal R=(A\widetilde h)\cdot\nabla_{\widetilde h}$. There is $\tau'>0$ such that $$\label{eq:source-15} \int_q^{2q} \frac{\mathbf E\|\mathcal R\widetilde\phi_p(\widetilde h_p)\|^2} {n^{2/3}p^2}\,d\log p \le C_A(q^{\tau'}+k_q^{-\tau'}).$$
  - Explanation: Unused Haar rotation converts angular derivatives into averaged posterior mean errors, giving a decaying angular score bound.
- **claim-exact-scale-conditional-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-variance-main)): There is a fixed $p_*>0$ with the following property. For every $\varepsilon>0$ there are finite constants $C$, $\xi>0$ and disorder events $\mathcal V_n$ with $\liminf_n\mathbb P(\mathcal V_n)\ge1-\varepsilon$ such that, on $\mathcal V_n$, simultaneously for every real function $f$ on the cube and every $r\le p\le p_*$, $$\label{eq:source-16} V_p(f):=Q_\mu[\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(f\mid h_p)] \le Cp^{-2}\mathcal E(f)+Ce^{-n^\xi}\\|f\\|_\infty^2.$$ The constants and the disorder event are independent of $f$. The inequality also holds componentwise and may be summed for vector-valued functions.
  - Explanation: The posterior variance is bounded by cap-scale inverse-square energy plus a negligible supremum-norm remainder. The same disorder event works for every test and scale, including vector components.
- **claim-positive-covariance-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-variance-curvature)): There are fixed $a,\xi,C>0$ and a sufficiently small fixed $p_*>0$ such that, for each deterministic $p\in[r,p_*]$ away from the activation scales, $$\label{eq:source-17} p^2K_p\preceq\bigl(1+C(p^a+k_p^{-a})\bigr)\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$$ outside an event $\mathcal N_{n,p}$ satisfying $\mathbf P(\mathcal N_{n,p}\cap\mathcal G_n)\le Ce^{-n^\xi}$. The exceptional event may depend on $p$; its probability bound has common constants for the entire scale range. By Fubini’s theorem the same bound holds after integration in $d\log p$, with a possibly smaller $\xi$.
  - Explanation: The positive covariance excess has a scale-integrable correction. The exceptional event may depend on the deterministic scale, but its probability bound is uniform across the scale range.
- **claim-annular-potential-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-variance-extension)): Choose $\eta>0$ sufficiently small compared with $\gamma^2$, then the coarse curvature tolerance $\varepsilon_0$ sufficiently small compared with $\eta$. The annulus width $s_0$ and the fixed upper scale may be chosen so that the following holds on $\mathcal G_n$. For every child field with $\|\alpha_c(h_c)\|\le s_0c^2$, the parent potential has an extension $\overline\phi_p$ with upper Hessian bound $(1+\eta)p^{-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$ that agrees with $\phi_p$ near $\|\alpha_p\|\le s_0p^2/2$. Under both the true transition and the transition obtained by using $\overline\phi_p$, the complement of this agreement region has probability at most $Ce^{-a_1k_p}$. The same bound holds with any fixed polynomial moment of $\\|h_p\\|/(p^2M_p)$ inserted, after decreasing $a_1>0$.
  - Explanation: An annular semiconcave extension preserves the true potential near agreement and supplies the Hessian bound needed for a strictly log-concave transition comparison.
- **claim-transverse-covariance-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-variance-radial)): On the coarse annulus, let $D=p^2K_p-\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$, $e=h_p/\\|h_p\\|$, $z=D_{\perp e}$, and define, on $e^\perp$, $$E_p=(D_{\perp\perp}+zz^{\mathsf T}/\|D_{ee}\|)_+,$$ extended by zero on $e$. Put $s_p^+=\\|D_+\\|$. Then $D\preceq E_p$ and $$\label{eq:source-19} \\|E_p\\|\le Cs_p^+, \qquad \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}E_p}{k_p} \le C\frac{\\|D\\|_{\mathrm F}}{\sqrt{k_p}}+\frac C{k_p}.$$
  - Explanation: The radial deficit permits a Schur-complement domination by a transverse positive operator whose norm and trace can be controlled.
- **claim-unused-frame-compression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-variance-compression)): For $x=c/p\in[\gamma,2\gamma]$, $$\label{eq:source-21} \\|P_cE_pP_c\\| \le C\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}E_p}{k_p}+x^3\\|E_p\\|\right)$$ outside a set of $\mathbf P$-probability at most $C\exp(-a_\gamma k_p)$. This is an unconditional assertion with respect to the unused spectral refinement; its exceptional set may afterwards be intersected with $\mathcal G_n$.
  - Explanation: Haar concentration bounds compression of the parent error into the smaller child subspace by its average trace and a dimension-ratio contribution.
- **claim-fixed-scale-variance-start** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-variance-start)): On disorder events of probability tending to one, for a sufficiently small fixed $p_{\mathrm{top}}>0$ and every real function $f$ on the cube, $$V_{p_{\mathrm{top}}}(f) \le C\mathcal E(f)+Ce^{-a n}\\|f\\|_\infty^2.$$ The event and constants are independent of $f$.
  - Explanation: A fixed-scale continuation to a product posterior gives a variance-energy starting inequality, uniformly over cube tests.
- **claim-conditioned-gradient-domain** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-gradient-domain)): The Euclidean gradient on smooth compact cylinder functions is closable from $L^2(\Pi)$ to $L^2(\Pi;\ell^2)$. Its closed graph consists exactly of the pairs satisfying <a href="#eq:source-23" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-23">[eq:source-23]</a>. In particular, the vector $G$ in that identity is unique; we denote it by $\nabla F$.* *The closed form $$\mathcal E_\Pi(F)=\int\|\nabla F\|^2\,d\Pi$$ has kernel equal to the constants. Each coordinate $x_a$ belongs to its domain and satisfies $\nabla x_a=e_a$, so that $\mathcal E_\Pi(x_a)=1$.
  - Explanation: Angular weak identities characterize exactly the closure of the cylinder gradient. The gradient is unique, the form kernel consists of constants, and coordinate functions belong to the domain.
- **claim-compensated-derivative-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gradient-compensated)): Let $\gamma=\bigotimes_{i\ge1}\mathsf N(0,l_i)$, where $l_i>0$, $\sum_i l_i=\infty$ and $\sum_i l_i^2<\infty$, and let $S_y=\sum_i(y_i^2-l_i)$. Suppose that $f,a\in L^2(\gamma)$ and $G\in L^2(\gamma;\ell^2)$ satisfy, coordinatewise in the weak sense, $$\partial_{y_i}f-2y_i a=G_i.$$ Then $(f,a,G)$ is a limit in these three spaces of triples $$\left(P,\partial_s P, \bigl(\partial_{y_i}P\bigr)_i\right)\Big\|_{s=S_y},$$ where $P=P(s,y_1,\ldots,y_N)$ is a polynomial. The derivatives in this display treat $s$ and the individual $y_i$ as independent variables.
  - Explanation: Weak compensated derivatives can be approximated in the full graph norm by polynomials in the centered square sum and finitely many individual coordinates.
- **claim-cap-smoothing-slopes** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gradient-smoothing)): For $r\le p\le p_*$ in the range of Proposition <a href="#prop:variance-main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:variance-main">16</a>, $$Q_\mu\\|\nabla_{u_p}F_p\\|^2 \le C T\mathcal E(f)+o(1), \label{eq:source-26}$$ and $$\begin{split} \\|n^{1/3}(f-S_pf)\\|_{L^2(\mu)} &\le Cp^{-1}\sqrt{T\mathcal E(f)}+o(1),\\ \left\\|n^{1/3}(D_iS_pf)_i-J_p^f\right\\|_{L^2(\mu;\mathbb R^n)} &\le Cp\sqrt{T\mathcal E(f)}+o(1). \end{split} \label{eq:source-27}$$ In particular, $J_p^f$ is bounded in $L^2(\mu;\mathbb R^n)$.
  - Explanation: Cap smoothing controls observation derivatives and approximates both values and discrete gradients with energy-dependent errors.
- **claim-observation-gradient-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-gradient-observation)): Suppose $f_n$ satisfies the preceding bounds and has weak cylinder limit $F$. Choose a subsequence on which $T\mathcal E(f_n)$ tends to its liminf. After further extraction in the quenched convention, there is a sequence $q_j\downarrow0$ such that the spectral components of $\overline{J^f}_{q_j}$ have weak cylinder limits $G^{(j)}$ as $n\to\infty$. Every weak cluster point of $(G^{(j)})$ in $L^2(\Pi;\ell^2)$ equals $\nabla F$. In particular, $F$ belongs to the form domain of Proposition <a href="#prop:gradient-domain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:gradient-domain">22</a> and $$\\|\nabla F\\|_{L^2(\Pi;\ell^2)} \le C\left(\liminf_{n\to\infty}T\mathcal E(f_n)\right)^{1/2}. \label{eq:source-28}$$ The constant is the same for all sequences with the stated size bounds.
  - Explanation: Weak limits of averaged observation slopes identify the closed conditioned gradient of the weak cylinder limit. A coarse energy bound ensures form-domain membership.
- **claim-microscopic-moment-measure** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-micro-moment-measure)): There is a deterministic finite positive measure $\omega$ on $[0,\infty)$ whose moments are given by <a href="#eq:source-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-3">[eq:source-3]</a>. For fixed leading indices $a,a'$, polynomials $Q,Q'$, and a bounded continuous cylinder $\psi$, $$\mu\!\left[\psi(x)\sum_iw_i(A_a^Q)_i(A_{a'}^{Q'})_i\right] \longrightarrow \mathbf 1_{\{a=a'\}}\Pi[\psi]\int QQ'\,d\omega. \label{eq:source-32}$$ For every $\varepsilon>0$ one also has $$\lim_{q\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb P\!\left( \mu\!\left[\left\\|P_q(wA_a^Q)-v_a\int Q\,d\omega\right\\|^2\right] >\varepsilon\right)=0. \label{eq:source-33}$$ Here $wA$ denotes entrywise multiplication. For some $c>0$, $\int e^{c\sqrt\lambda}\,d\omega(\lambda)<\infty$. The measure is uniquely determined by its moments, and polynomials are dense in $L^2((1+\lambda)\omega+\delta_0)$.
  - Explanation: Fixed microscopic contractions define a deterministic positive moment measure. It determines corrector slope limits, has a square-root exponential moment, and admits the required polynomial approximation.
- **claim-connected-haar-contractions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-micro-contractions)): Suppose the edge matrices have uniformly bounded operator norms and that the traces and paths in <a href="#eq:micro-spectral-words" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:micro-spectral-words">[eq:micro-spectral-words]</a> converge for every fixed word. For a fixed connected $G$ and fixed continuous vertex functions of polynomial growth, $S_G$ converges in every fixed $L^p$ under the conditional Haar law to a deterministic limit. The same holds for the sum restricted to injective site labels. Products of finitely many such contractions have the products of their limits.* *The conclusion applies to the contractions defining $\sum_iw_i(A_a^Q)_i(A_{a'}^{Q'})_i$ at the sampled spin, and to their quadratic contractions with an additional $P_q$ edge, for $q>0$ fixed. In the latter case the limit tends to zero as $q\downarrow0$ if that edge is replaced by $P_q-P_{\rm mark}$, where $P_{\rm mark}$ projects onto the marked spectral axes.
  - Explanation: Connected graph contractions have deterministic conditional Haar limits and factorize across products. An extra cap edge outside the marked axes vanishes in the successive limits.
- **claim-weighted-difference-recursion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-micro-weighted-recursion)): Fix $K<\infty$. Suppose that $W$ is symmetric, has zero diagonal, and obeys $$\\|W\\|\le K,\qquad \epsilon_n:=\max_{i,j}\|W_{ij}\|\le n^{-49/100}.$$ There is $C=C(K)\ge1$ such that, for each fixed integer $m\ge1$, uniformly in these matrices and in real functions $f$ on $\{-1,1\}^n$, $$N_m(Hf)\le\sum_{l=1}^{m+2} \bigl(C^{m-l+3}(m+1)^{m-l+2}+o_{n;m}(1)\bigr)N_l(f). \label{eq:source-35}$$ Here $o_{n;m}(1)$ is deterministic, nonnegative, and tends to zero as $n\to\infty$ with $m$ fixed.
  - Explanation: A deterministic weighted difference recursion controls application of the heat-bath generator using operator-norm and small-entry hypotheses, with the derivative order fixed before the size limit.
- **claim-moment-determinacy-polynomial-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-micro-moment-density)): Let the matrices and normalized linear functions above vary with $n$, and suppose the moments of the finite measures $$\omega_{n,a}(B)=\langle Y_a,H\mathbf1_B(H)Y_a\rangle_{L^2(\mu)}, \qquad B\subset[0,\infty)\text{ Borel},$$ converge at every fixed nonnegative integer order. They converge weakly to a finite measure $\omega$ on $[0,\infty)$ with those moments. This measure is uniquely determined among measures on $[0,\infty)$ by its moments, and, for some $c>0$, $$\int e^{c\sqrt\lambda}\,d\omega(\lambda)<\infty.$$ Polynomials are dense in both $L^2(\omega)$ and $L^2((1+\lambda)\omega+\delta_0)$.
  - Explanation: Convergent finite-system energy moments identify a unique weak limit. Analyticity of the symmetric square-root lift yields moment determinacy and polynomial density in the weighted spaces.
- **claim-cylinder-corrector-recovery** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-micro-recovery)): Let $F$ be a smooth compact cylinder, and let $Q$ be a polynomial with $Q(0)=1$. Define $$f_{n,Q}(X)=F(x)+n^{-1/3}\sum_aF_{,a}(x)(Q(H)-1)Y_a, \label{eq:source-39}$$ where the sum is over the finitely many coordinates of $F$. Then $f_{n,Q}$ is bounded by a fixed power of $n$, converges weakly against cylinders to $F$, and its squared $L^2$ norm converges to $\Pi[F^2]$. Moreover, $$\lim_{n\to\infty}T\mathcal E(f_{n,Q}) =\left(\int Q^2\,d\omega\right)\Pi[\\|\nabla F\\|^2].$$ The exponent in the polynomial bound may depend on $Q$.
  - Explanation: A generator-polynomial microscopic correction vanishes in the value norm while changing the limiting energy to the polynomial energy integral.
- **claim-effective-coefficient-positive** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-micro-positivity)): One has $c_*=\omega(\{0\})>0$.
  - Explanation: The coarse gradient bound and cylinder recovery force the microscopic energy measure to have a strictly positive atom at zero.
- **claim-corrector-multiplied-divergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-micro-divergence)): For a fixed polynomial $Q$, fixed leading index $a$, and smooth compact cylinder $\psi$, $$D_w^*(\psi(x)A_a^Q) =\psi(x)HQ(H)Y_a+o_{L^2(\mu)}(1), \qquad \\|HQ(H)Y_a\\|_2^2\longrightarrow\int\lambda Q^2\,d\omega.$$
  - Explanation: Multiplication by a smooth cylinder introduces only a vanishing divergence error. The remaining microscopic divergence norm is determined by the energy moment measure.
- **claim-relaxed-dirichlet-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-micro-relaxation)): The constant $c_*=\omega(\{0\})$ is deterministic and strictly positive. On any quenched represented subsequence, suppose that $\\|f_n\\|_\infty\le Cn^D$ for fixed $C,D$, that the $L^2$ norms and $T\mathcal E(f_n)$ are bounded, and that $f_n$ converges weakly against cylinders to $F$. Then $F$ is in the closed form domain on $L^2(\Pi)$ and $$\liminf_{n\to\infty}T\mathcal E(f_n) \ge c_*\Pi[\\|\nabla F\\|_{\ell^2}^2]. \label{eq:source-38}$$ Conversely, for each smooth compact cylinder $F$, the functions $f_{n,Q_j}$ in <a href="#eq:source-39" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-39">[eq:source-39]</a> converge strongly to $F$ for every fixed $j$, are polynomially bounded at that fixed $j$, and satisfy $$\lim_{j\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}T\mathcal E(f_{n,Q_j}) =c_*\Pi[\\|\nabla F\\|^2].$$ All assertions for countably many chosen sources and tests hold on a common represented event. In particular the lower bound and recovery use the same deterministic coefficient.
  - Explanation: The same positive deterministic coefficient gives the sharp lower energy bound and cylinder recovery limit. The microscopic polynomial limit follows the finite-size limit.
- **claim-gaussian-resolvent-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-trace-goe)): For almost every edge configuration $g$, the series defining $B_g(z)$ is finite for every $z>0$. For every finite collection $z_1,\ldots,z_k>0$, $$(B_n(z_1),\ldots,B_n(z_k)) \ \Longrightarrow\ (B_g(z_1),\ldots,B_g(z_k)).$$ Moreover, for each $z,\epsilon>0$, $$\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb P\left\{ \sum_{a>\ell}\langle x_a,(z+TH)^{-1}x_a\rangle_\mu >\epsilon\right\}=0.$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian resolvent traces converge jointly, and the high-coordinate trace tail vanishes in the successive size and coordinate-cutoff limits.
- **claim-fixed-coordinate-resolvents** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-fixed)): For every fixed $\ell$ and every finite collection of positive $z$, the corresponding sums over $1\leq a\leq\ell$ converge jointly to the same restrictions of <a href="#eq:source-40" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-40">[eq:source-40]</a>.
  - Explanation: The lower form bound and cylinder recovery identify resolvent convergence for every fixed coordinate block.
- **claim-vector-observation-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-replacement)): Uniformly over vector tests satisfying $\\|f\\|_{z,n}\leq1$ and a fixed polynomial supremum bound, $$\sum_{a>\ell}\mu[f_ax_a] =n^{-1/3}\sum_{a>\ell} Q_\mu\big[(F_r)_a(U^tm_r)_a\big]+o_{\mathbb P}(1). \label{eq:source-41}$$ The error is uniform in $\ell$ and in the number of vector components.
  - Explanation: Vector pairings with edge coordinates can be moved to the smallest observation cap, uniformly over the allowed variational tests and target dimensions.
- **claim-gaussian-quadratic-trace-vanishes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-trace-second)): For Gaussian disorder and every $z>0$, $$B_n^{[2]}(z)\longrightarrow0 \quad\text{in probability}. \label{eq:source-43}$$
  - Explanation: The Gaussian quadratic-feature trace vanishes because posterior replica overlaps are small after the smallest cap observation.
- **claim-sign-trace-universality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-comparison-universality)): For every $z>0$, $$\sup_{0\le t\le1}\mathbb E_t B_n^{[2]}(z)\longrightarrow0.$$ For each finite collection $z_1,\ldots,z_m>0$, the vector $(B_n(z_1),\ldots,B_n(z_m))$ under sign disorder converges in law to the Gaussian limiting vector in <a href="#eq:source-40" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-40">[eq:source-40]</a>. In particular the coefficient $c_*$ in that limit is unchanged.
  - Explanation: Quadratic traces vanish uniformly along the coupling mixture. This enables transfer of the spin resolvent trace law to sign disorder with the same microscopic time coefficient.
- **claim-mixture-static-overlap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-overlap)): For every $0<\epsilon\le1$ there is $a_\epsilon>0$ such that $$\label{eq:comparison-overlap-tail} \sup_{0\le t\le1}\mathbb P_t\left\{ \mu^{\otimes2}(\|\rho\|\ge\epsilon)>e^{-a_\epsilon n} \right\}\longrightarrow0.$$ Consequently, for fixed $C,K<\infty$, uniformly over nonnegative, possibly disorder-dependent functions $p$ satisfying $\mu p\le1$ and $\\|p\\|_\infty\le Cn^K$, the quantities $$\frac1{\binom n2}\sum_{i<j}\big\|\mu[pX_iX_j]\big\|$$ converge to zero in probability, uniformly in $t$. They also converge to zero in expectation, uniformly in $t$.
  - Explanation: Macroscopic replica overlaps are suppressed uniformly along the mixture. Polynomially bounded nonnegative reweightings then have vanishing average pair correlations.
- **claim-pair-residual-sensitivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-residual)): Uniformly over the disorder and the pair $e$, $$\label{eq:source-44} R_e^2\le\frac{2T}{z}\Gamma_e.$$ For $S=S^{[2]}$ one also has $R_e^2\le C_z$, whereas for $S=S^{[1]}$, $$\label{eq:source-45} R_e^2\le C_z\bigl(1+n^{1/3}\sqrt{B_n^{[2]}(z)}\bigr).$$ Consequently $\operatorname{avg}_eR_e^2\le C_z n^{-1/3}$. Its fourth-power analogue is $\operatorname{avg}_eR_e^4\le C_z n^{-1/3}$ for $S^{[2]}$, and $$\label{eq:source-46} \operatorname{avg}_eR_e^4 \le C_z\bigl(n^{-1/3}+\sqrt{B_n^{[2]}(z)}\bigr) \qquad\text{for }S^{[1]}.$$
  - Explanation: A pair perturbation touches only endpoint energies. Its residual sensitivity has small pair averages, with the linear-feature fourth-power bound controlled by the quadratic trace.
- **claim-averaged-feature-sensitivities** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-average)): For either feature, $\operatorname{avg}_e(h_e+h_e^4)\le C_z$ deterministically. There is a deterministic sequence $\epsilon_n\to0$ such that, uniformly $t\in[0,1]$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_t\operatorname{avg}_e(h_e+h_e^4)&\le\epsilon_n &&\text{for }S^{[2]},\label{eq:comparison-average-second}\\ \mathbb E_t\operatorname{avg}_e(h_e+h_e^4)&\le\epsilon_n+ C_z\mathbb E_t\sqrt{B_n^{[2]}(z)} &&\text{for }S^{[1]}. \label{eq:comparison-average-first} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Static overlap control and endpoint-energy counting make the average sensitivity small. The linear feature requires the additional quadratic-trace term.
- **claim-compactified-trace-fourth-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-derivative)): Fix finitely many traces $B_a=B_n^{[k_a]}(z_a)$, with $k_a\in\{1,2\}$ and $z_a>0$, and a smooth function $F$ on a neighborhood of $[0,1]^m$. For an arbitrary base disorder and pair $e$, let $B_a(\theta)$ denote the traces after adding $\theta$ to the interaction coefficient of $e$, and let $\mathfrak h_e=\sum_{a=1}^m h_{e,a}$, with each sensitivity computed at the base disorder. Then for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{eq:comparison-fourth-derivative} \sup_{\|\theta\|\le2\log n/\sqrt n} \left\|\frac{d^4}{d\theta^4} F\left(\frac1{1+B_1(\theta)},\ldots, \frac1{1+B_m(\theta)}\right)\right\| \le C_F(\mathfrak h_e+\mathfrak h_e^4).$$ At every real base disorder, derivatives of orders at most four also have a deterministic bound polynomial in $n$.
  - Explanation: Compactifying the resolvent traces permits a fourth-derivative bound in terms of pair sensitivities along the replacement segment.
- **claim-stieltjes-laplace-functional-continuity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-functional-spectral)): Let $\nu_n$ and $\nu$ be random nonnegative measures on $[0,\infty)$ such that their integrals against $(1+\lambda)^{-1}$ are finite almost surely, for every $n$ and for $\nu$. Suppose the corresponding integrals against $(z+\lambda)^{-1}$ converge jointly in law for every finite collection of positive $z$, including $z=1$. Then $$\left(s\longmapsto\int e^{-s\lambda}\,d\nu_n(\lambda)\right) \ \Longrightarrow\ \left(s\longmapsto\int e^{-s\lambda}\,d\nu(\lambda)\right)$$ in $C_{\rm loc}((0,\infty))$.
  - Explanation: Joint convergence of weighted Stieltjes transforms identifies the random spectral measure and yields compact-positive-time convergence of its Laplace transform.
- **claim-common-functional-limit-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-functional-identification)): The series <a href="#eq:source-2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-2">[eq:source-2]</a> has a Borel version $g\mapsto\mathcal A_g$ with values in $C_{\rm loc}((0,\infty))$. For both Gaussian and symmetric-sign entries, $$\operatorname{Law}(A_{n,J})\Longrightarrow Q_{\rm crit}=\operatorname{Law}(\mathcal A_g),$$ where the same edge law and the same deterministic coefficient $c_*$ are used in <a href="#eq:source-2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-2">[eq:source-2]</a>.
  - Explanation: The infinite coordinate autocorrelation series has a measurable continuous-function version and gives the common Gaussian and sign functional limit.
- **claim-edge-finite-shift-absolute-continuity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-functional-shift)): For every fixed $m$ and $h\in\mathbb R^m$, $$(T_h)_*(\mathsf P\|_{\mathcal O_h})\ll\mathsf P.$$ In particular, lowering $g_1$ by any fixed positive amount gives a law absolutely continuous with respect to $\mathsf P$.
  - Explanation: Finite shifts of the edge points preserve absolute continuity when strict ordering is retained. Lowering the leading point is an admissible special case.
- **claim-positive-time-essential-unboundedness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-functional-randomness)): For every fixed $s>0$, the finite random variable $\mathcal A_g(s)$ is essentially unbounded. In particular $Q_{\rm crit}$ is not a point mass.
  - Explanation: Finite edge shifts and exponential tilting force the leading coordinate variance to be essentially unbounded, so every fixed positive-time limiting autocorrelation is also essentially unbounded.
- **claim-constant-function-in-closed-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-support-constant)): The constant function $s\mapsto 1$ belongs to the closed topological support of $Q_{\rm crit}$ in $C_{\rm loc}((0,\infty))$.
  - Explanation: Finite ordered edge modifications produce autocorrelation functions approaching the constant function in the local topology. This is compatible with almost-sure decay because decay at infinity is not a closed condition in that topology.

        "exact_quote": "Under $\\mathbf P$, $U^{\\mathsf T}X$ has exactly the spherical Gibbs law in spectral coordinates. After the row sign change sending $X$ to $\\mathbf1$, conditional on $y=U^{\\mathsf T}X$ the eigenframe is Haar subject to $Uy=\\mathbf1$. Moreover, on the eigenvalue events used below, $$\\label{eq:edge-uncapped-comparison}\n E_U(L_\\circ-1)^2=o(1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-227.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-edge-cap-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite field radius $R$ and positive fixed error tolerances. There are sufficiently small constants $p_{\\rm top},s_0>0$ such that the following hold with probability tending to one in the range $np^3\\ge c\\sqrt{\\log n}$ and $p\\le p_{\\rm top}$.*\n\n**Spectral and spherical estimates, at every scale.* The spectral dimension satisfies $\\dim\\mathcal H_p\\asymp k_p$, $d_1\\ge-o(p^2)$ uniformly in the cap range, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}R_{p,0}^2=O(n/p)$. On $|\\alpha|\\le s_0p^2$, $\\ell_p(\\alpha)\\asymp p^2M_p$ and $\\ell_p'(\\alpha)\\asymp M_p$. For $\\|h\\|\\le Rp^2M_p$, the saddle precision has gap comparable to $p^2$. At $\\alpha=\\alpha_p(h)$, the density at $n$ of the squared norm of $\\mathsf N(R_{p,\\alpha}h,R_{p,\\alpha})$ is comparable to $\\sqrt{p/n}$. After multiplication by $e^{v^{\\mathsf T}X}$ and renormalization, the Gaussian mean becomes $R_{p,\\alpha}(h+v)$ while its covariance remains $R_{p,\\alpha}$; its squared-norm density at $n$ is at most a fixed multiple of $\\sqrt{p/n}$. For $m$ replicas, where $m$ is fixed, consider rowwise linear or quadratic tilts that preserve Gaussianity. The joint density of their squared norms is at most $C_m(p/n)^{m/2}$ under the following hypotheses. The spectral rows remain independent after the rowwise tilt, the tilted precision is positive definite, and on at least $ck_p$ rows the post-tilt row covariance $C_a$ satisfies $c p^{-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}_m\\preceq C_a\\preceq C p^{-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}_m$. All comparison constants here are fixed.*\n\n\n**Curvature estimates, at every scale.* On the annulus, for any prescribed fixed $\\epsilon_0>0$, $$\\label{eq:source-6}\n K_p\\preceq(1+\\epsilon_0)p^{-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}_{\\mathcal H_p},\\qquad\n p^2(K_p)_{ee}\\le1-\\epsilon_1,\n \\quad e=h/\\|h\\|,\\quad \\epsilon_1=0.1.$$ On the entire field ball one also has $K_p\\preceq C_Rp^{-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}_{\\mathcal H_p}$, where $C_R$ may be chosen before $\\epsilon_0$.*\n\n**Static comparisons and cube projection tails, on geometric lists.* For any prescribed fixed geometric list of scales, uniformly over the field ball, $$|\\phi_p(h)-\\phi_p^{\\rm sp}(h)|\\le\\epsilon_v k_p,\n \\qquad\n \\|P_pm_p(h)-R_{p,\\alpha_p(h)}h\\|\\le\\epsilon_gM_p.$$ Any fixed finite family of such lists is allowed. For each $h\\in\\mathcal H_p$ with $\\|h\\|\\le Rp^2M_p$, the cube posterior $\\mu_{p,h}$ satisfies, uniformly for every $v\\ge v_0$ with a sufficiently large fixed threshold $v_0$, $$\\mu_{p,h}\\{\\|P_pX\\|>vM_p\\}\\le C\\exp(-ak_pv^6).$$ For $v>p^{-1/2}$ the event is empty, since $\\|X\\|=\\sqrt n$. Here $a>0$, and the same bound holds under the uncapped zero-field cube measure $\\mu$ at the same list scales.*\n\n*The spherical projection-tail estimate holds at every scale in the stated range, for capped bounded-field and uncapped zero-field measures. Also, at every such cap scale, for independent spherical replicas at a common field in the prescribed ball, $$(\\mu_{p,h}^{\\rm sp})^{\\otimes2}\n \\{|n^{-1}X^{\\mathsf T}X'|>\\rho\\}\n \\le C\\exp(-cn\\rho^3),\\qquad Cp\\le\\rho\\le\\rho_0,$$ for a sufficiently small fixed $\\rho_0>0$. A fixed finite number of replicas obeys the corresponding bound for any pair.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gradient-domain",
        "exact_quote": "The Euclidean gradient on smooth compact cylinder functions is closable from $L^2(\\Pi)$ to $L^2(\\Pi;\\ell^2)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gradient-compensated",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma=\\bigotimes_{i\\ge1}\\mathsf N(0,l_i)$, where $l_i>0$, $\\sum_i l_i=\\infty$ and $\\sum_i l_i^2<\\infty$, and let $S_y=\\sum_i(y_i^2-l_i)$. Suppose that $f,a\\in L^2(\\gamma)$ and $G\\in L^2(\\gamma;\\ell^2)$ satisfy, coordinatewise in the weak sense, $$\\partial_{y_i}f-2y_i a=G_i.$$ Then $(f,a,G)$ is a limit in these three spaces of triples $$\\left(P,\\partial_s P,\n       \\bigl(\\partial_{y_i}P\\bigr)_i\\right)\\Big|_{s=S_y},$$ where $P=P(s,y_1,\\ldots,y_N)$ is a polynomial.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gradient-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "For $r\\le p\\le p_*$ in the range of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:variance-main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:variance-main\">16</a>, $$Q_\\mu\\|\\nabla_{u_p}F_p\\|^2\n       \\le C T\\mathcal E(f)+o(1),\n \\label{eq:source-26}$$ and $$\\begin{split}\n \\|n^{1/3}(f-S_pf)\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}\n       &\\le Cp^{-1}\\sqrt{T\\mathcal E(f)}+o(1),\\\\\n \\left\\|n^{1/3}(D_iS_pf)_i-J_p^f\\right\\|_{L^2(\\mu;\\mathbb R^n)}\n       &\\le Cp\\sqrt{T\\mathcal E(f)}+o(1).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:source-27}$$ In particular, $J_p^f$ is bounded in $L^2(\\mu;\\mathbb R^n)$.",

          "source_id": "PM-227.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gradient-observation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $f_n$ satisfies the preceding bounds and has weak cylinder limit $F$. Choose a subsequence on which $T\\mathcal E(f_n)$ tends to its liminf. After further extraction in the quenched convention, there is a sequence $q_j\\downarrow0$ such that the spectral components of $\\overline{J^f}_{q_j}$ have weak cylinder limits $G^{(j)}$ as $n\\to\\infty$. Every weak cluster point of $(G^{(j)})$ in $L^2(\\Pi;\\ell^2)$ equals $\\nabla F$. In particular, $F$ belongs to the form domain of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:gradient-domain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:gradient-domain\">22</a> and $$\\|\\nabla F\\|_{L^2(\\Pi;\\ell^2)}\n \\le C\\left(\\liminf_{n\\to\\infty}T\\mathcal E(f_n)\\right)^{1/2}.\n \\label{eq:source-28}$$ The constant is the same for all sequences with the stated size bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gradient-observation",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $f_n$ satisfies the preceding bounds and has weak cylinder limit $F$. Choose a subsequence on which $T\\mathcal E(f_n)$ tends to its liminf. Every weak cluster point of $(G^{(j)})$ in $L^2(\\Pi;\\ell^2)$ equals $\\nabla F$. In particular, $F$ belongs to the form domain of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:gradient-domain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:gradient-domain\">22</a> and $$\\|\\nabla F\\|_{L^2(\\Pi;\\ell^2)}\n \\le C\\left(\\liminf_{n\\to\\infty}T\\mathcal E(f_n)\\right)^{1/2}.\n \\label{eq:source-28}$$ The constant is the same for all sequences with the stated size bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-227.2"

      "exact_statement": "Suppose $f_n$ satisfies the preceding bounds and has weak cylinder limit $F$. Choose a subsequence on which $T\\mathcal E(f_n)$ tends to its liminf.         "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic finite positive measure $\\omega$ on $[0,\\infty)$ whose moments are given by <a href=\"#eq:source-3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:source-3\">[eq:source-3]</a>. For fixed leading indices $a,a'$, polynomials $Q,Q'$, and a bounded continuous cylinder $\\psi$, $$\\mu\\!\\left[\\psi(x)\\sum_iw_i(A_a^Q)_i(A_{a'}^{Q'})_i\\right]\n \\longrightarrow\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{a=a'\\}}\\Pi[\\psi]\\int QQ'\\,d\\omega.\n \\label{eq:source-32}$$ For every $\\varepsilon>0$ one also has $$\\lim_{q\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n \\mathbb P\\!\\left(\n  \\mu\\!\\left[\\left\\|P_q(wA_a^Q)-v_a\\int Q\\,d\\omega\\right\\|^2\\right]\n      >\\varepsilon\\right)=0.\n \\label{eq:source-33}$$ Here $wA$ denotes entrywise multiplication. For some $c>0$, $\\int e^{c\\sqrt\\lambda}\\,d\\omega(\\lambda)<\\infty$. The measure is uniquely determined by its moments, and polynomials are dense in $L^2((1+\\lambda)\\omega+\\delta_0)$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-micro-contractions",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the edge matrices have uniformly bounded operator norms and that the traces and paths in <a href=\"#eq:micro-spectral-words\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:micro-spectral-words\">[eq:micro-spectral-words]</a> converge for every fixed word. For a fixed connected $G$ and fixed continuous vertex functions of polynomial growth, $S_G$ converges in every fixed $L^p$ under the conditional Haar law to a deterministic limit. The same holds for the sum restricted to injective site labels. Products of finitely many such contractions have the products of their limits.*\n\n*The conclusion applies to the contractions defining $\\sum_iw_i(A_a^Q)_i(A_{a'}^{Q'})_i$ at the sampled spin, and to their quadratic contractions with an additional $P_q$ edge, for $q>0$ fixed. In the latter case the limit tends to zero as $q\\downarrow0$ if that edge is replaced by $P_q-P_{\\rm mark}$, where $P_{\\rm mark}$ projects onto the marked spectral axes.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-micro-weighted-recursion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $K<\\infty$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-micro-moment-density",
        "exact_quote": "Let the matrices and normalized linear functions above vary with $n$, and suppose the moments of the finite measures $$\\omega_{n,a}(B)=\\langle Y_a,H\\mathbf1_B(H)Y_a\\rangle_{L^2(\\mu)},\n \\qquad B\\subset[0,\\infty)\\text{ Borel},$$ converge at every fixed nonnegative integer order. They converge weakly to a finite measure $\\omega$ on $[0,\\infty)$ with those moments. This measure is uniquely determined among measures on $[0,\\infty)$ by its moments, and, for some $c>0$, $$\\int e^{c\\sqrt\\lambda}\\,d\\omega(\\lambda)<\\infty.$$ Polynomials are dense in both $L^2(\\omega)$ and $L^2((1+\\lambda)\\omega+\\delta_0)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-micro-recovery",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a smooth compact cylinder, and let $Q$ be a polynomial with $Q(0)=1$. Define $$f_{n,Q}(X)=F(x)+n^{-1/3}\\sum_aF_{,a}(x)(Q(H)-1)Y_a,\n \\label{eq:source-39}$$ where the sum is over the finitely many coordinates of $F$. Then $f_{n,Q}$ is bounded by a fixed power of $n$, converges weakly against cylinders to $F$, and its squared $L^2$ norm converges to $\\Pi[F^2]$. Moreover, $$\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}T\\mathcal E(f_{n,Q})\n       =\\left(\\int Q^2\\,d\\omega\\right)\\Pi[\\|\\nabla F\\|^2].$$ The exponent in the polynomial bound may depend on $Q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-micro-recovery",

Then $f_{n,Q}$ is bounded by a fixed power of $n$, converges weakly against cylinders to $F$, and its squared $L^2$ norm converges to $\\Pi[F^2]$.           "source_id": "PM-227.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-micro-relaxation",
        "exact_quote": "The constant $c_*=\\omega(\\{0\\})$ is deterministic and strictly positive. On any quenched represented subsequence, suppose that $\\|f_n\\|_\\infty\\le Cn^D$ for fixed $C,D$, that the $L^2$ norms and $T\\mathcal E(f_n)$ are bounded, and that $f_n$ converges weakly against cylinders to $F$. Then $F$ is in the closed form domain on $L^2(\\Pi)$ and $$\\liminf_{n\\to\\infty}T\\mathcal E(f_n)\n        \\ge c_*\\Pi[\\|\\nabla F\\|_{\\ell^2}^2].\n \\label{eq:source-38}$$ Conversely, for each smooth compact cylinder $F$, the functions $f_{n,Q_j}$ in <a href=\"#eq:source-39\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:source-39\">[eq:source-39]</a> converge strongly to $F$ for every fixed $j$, are polynomially bounded at that fixed $j$, and satisfy $$\\lim_{j\\to\\infty}\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}T\\mathcal E(f_{n,Q_j})\n                       =c_*\\Pi[\\|\\nabla F\\|^2].$$ All assertions for countably many chosen sources and tests hold on a common represented event. In particular the lower bound and recovery use the same deterministic coefficient.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-comparison-derivative",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many traces $B_a=B_n^{[k_a]}(z_a)$, with $k_a\\in\\{1,2\\}$ and $z_a>0$, and a smooth function $F$ on a neighborhood of $[0,1]^m$. For an arbitrary base disorder and pair $e$, let $B_a(\\theta)$ denote the traces after adding $\\theta$ to the interaction coefficient of $e$, and let $\\mathfrak h_e=\\sum_{a=1}^m h_{e,a}$, with each sensitivity computed at the base disorder. Then for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{eq:comparison-fourth-derivative}\n \\sup_{|\\theta|\\le2\\log n/\\sqrt n}\n \\left|\\frac{d^4}{d\\theta^4}\n F\\left(\\frac1{1+B_1(\\theta)},\\ldots,\n        \\frac1{1+B_m(\\theta)}\\right)\\right|\n \\le C_F(\\mathfrak h_e+\\mathfrak h_e^4).$$ At every real base disorder, derivatives of orders at most four also have a deterministic bound polynomial in $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-comparison-derivative",

Then for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\label{eq:comparison-fourth-derivative}\n \\sup_{|\\theta|\\le2\\log n/\\sqrt n}\n \\left|\\frac{d^4}{d\\theta^4}\n F\\left(\\frac1{1+B_1(\\theta)},\\ldots,\n        \\frac1{1+B_m(\\theta)}\\right)\\right|\n \\le C_F(\\mathfrak h_e+\\mathfrak h_e^4).$$ At every real base disorder, derivatives of orders at most four also have a deterministic bound polynomial in $n$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-functional-spectral",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu_n$ and $\\nu$ be random nonnegative measures on $[0,\\infty)$ such that their integrals against $(1+\\lambda)^{-1}$ are finite almost surely, for every $n$ and for $\\nu$. Suppose the corresponding integrals against $(z+\\lambda)^{-1}$ converge jointly in law for every finite collection of positive $z$, including $z=1$. Then $$\\left(s\\longmapsto\\int e^{-s\\lambda}\\,d\\nu_n(\\lambda)\\right)\n \\ \\Longrightarrow\\\n \\left(s\\longmapsto\\int e^{-s\\lambda}\\,d\\nu(\\lambda)\\right)$$ in $C_{\\rm loc}((0,\\infty))$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu_n$ and $\\nu$ be random nonnegative measures on $[0,\\infty)$ such that their integrals against $(1+\\lambda)^{-1}$ are finite almost surely, for every $n$ and for $\\nu$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Critical zero-field Gibbs measure** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $n\ge2$, let $(J_{ij})_{i<j}$ be independent with law $D$, put $J_{ji}=J_{ij}$ and $J_{ii}=0$, and define $$\label{eq:intro-gibbs} \pi_{n,J}(\sigma)=\frac1{Z_{n,J}} \exp\left(\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i<j}J_{ij}\sigma_i\sigma_j\right), \qquad \sigma\in\{-1,1\}^n.$$
- **Stationary autocorrelation and critical rescaling** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its stationary spin autocorrelation and its critical rescaling are $$\begin{aligned} C_{n,J}(t)&=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n \langle\sigma_i,P_t^J\sigma_i\rangle_{\pi_{n,J}}, \label{eq:intro-correlation}\\ A_{n,J}(s)&=n^{1/3}C_{n,J}(s n^{2/3}),\qquad s>0. \label{eq:intro-rescaling} \end{aligned}$$
- **Local continuous-function topology** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $C_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,\infty))$ denote the continuous real functions, with uniform convergence on every compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$.
- **Heat-bath operator and Dirichlet form** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The nonnegative heat-bath operator and its Dirichlet form are $$\label{eq:edge-heatbath-form} H=\sum_{i=1}^n(X_i-t_i)D_i, \qquad \mathcal E(f,g)=\mu\!\left[\sum_{i=1}^n w_iD_ifD_ig\right], \qquad \mathcal E(f)=\mathcal E(f,f).$$
- **Edge coordinates and finite resolvent trace** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Set $$Y_a=v_a^{\mathsf T}X,\qquad x_a=n^{-1/3}Y_a, \qquad B_n(z)=\sum_{a=1}^n\langle x_a,(z+TH)^{-1}x_a\rangle_\mu, \quad z>0.$$
- **Renormalized quadratic constraint** ([source](#eq:source-1)): Define $$\label{eq:source-1} D(b)=\lim_{L\to\infty}\left\{ \sqrt L-\sum_{a\ge1}\left(\frac1{g_a+b}-\frac1{g_a+L}\right)\right\}.$$
- **Conditioned edge measure** ([source](#stmt-prop-edge-conditioned-law)): The law of $(Z_a)_{a\ge1}$ conditioned on $S=D(b)$ has a version $\Pi=\Pi_g$ characterized by continuous conditional densities.
- **Spectral cap** ([source](#pm-h-10)): The cap at scale $p>0$ is defined by $$P_p=\mathbf 1_{\{d\le p^2\}},\quad \mathcal H_p=\operatorname{ran}P_p,\quad B_p=(p^2-d)_+,\quad W_p=W-B_p,\quad k_p=np^3,\quad M_p=\sqrt{np}.$$
- **Posterior potential, mean, and covariance** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Let $\phi_p(h)$ be its log partition function, $m_p(h)=\nabla\phi_p(h)$ its mean, and $K_p(h)=P_p\nabla^2\phi_p(h)P_p$ its covariance restricted to $\mathcal H_p$.
- **Smooth cylinder function** ([source](#pm-h-21)): A *smooth cylinder function* is a smooth function of finitely many coordinates.
- **Angular weak gradient identities** ([source](#eq:source-23)): We say that $(F,G)\in L^2(\Pi)\times L^2(\Pi;\ell^2)$ satisfies the angular weak identities if $$\int \psi\,(Ax)\cdot G\,d\Pi =-\int F\left\{\mathcal R_A\psi -\psi\sum_a (Ax)_a(g_a+b)x_a\right\}\,d\Pi \label{eq:source-23}$$ for every such $A$ and every smooth compact cylinder test $\psi$ whose coordinate set contains the indices of $A$.
- **Limiting gradient form** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a smooth compactly supported cylinder function $F$ on $\mathbb R^{\mathbb N}$, consider the quadratic form $$\mathcal E_\Pi(F)=\int\sum_a\|\partial_aF\|^2\,d\Pi.$$
- **Microscopic energy measure** ([source](#pm-h-32)): Let $\nu_{a,n}$ be the spectral measure of $H$ on $Y_a$, and put $\omega_{a,n}(d\lambda)=\lambda\,\nu_{a,n}(d\lambda)$.
- **Microscopic effective coefficient** ([source](#pm-h-35)): Define $c_*=\omega(\{0\})$.
- **Limiting autocorrelation** ([source](#eq:source-2)): The law in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is specified by $$\label{eq:source-2} \mathcal A(s)=\sum_{a\ge1} \langle x_a,e^{-s c_*H_\Pi}x_a\rangle_\Pi, \qquad s>0,$$ where $c_*>0$ is deterministic.
- **Limiting resolvent trace** ([source](#eq:source-40)): Write $$B_g(z):=\sum_{a\geq1} \big\langle x_a,(z+c_*H_\Pi)^{-1}x_a\big\rangle_\Pi, \qquad z>0. \label{eq:source-40}$$
- **Quadratic spin feature trace** ([source](#pm-h-39)): Regard $XX^t/n$ as a vector in the space of matrices with the Hilbert–Schmidt inner product, and set $$B_n^{[2]}(z)=\sum_{i,j=1}^n \left\langle\frac{X_iX_j}{n}, (z+TH)^{-1}\frac{X_iX_j}{n}\right\rangle_\mu.$$

      "exact_definition": "Regard $XX^t/n$ as a vector in the space of matrices with the Hilbert–Schmidt inner product, and set $$B_n^{[2]}(z)=\\sum_{i,j=1}^n\n \\left\\langle\\frac{X_iX_j}{n},\n (z+TH)^{-1}\\frac{X_iX_j}{n}\\right\\rangle_\\mu.$$",
      "interpretation": "The normalized spin-product matrix produces the bounded auxiliary trace that controls coupling-comparison remainders.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Quadratic spin feature trace",
      "scope": "Finite-system disorder comparison."
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-227.2",
  "schema": "pm.objects.v2"
}

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-time-scaling | Autocorrelation time multiplier | = | $n^{2/3}$ | System size tends to infinity with positive rescaled time fixed. |
| q-critical-amplitude-scaling | Autocorrelation amplitude multiplier | = | $n^{1/3}$ | Positive rescaled times and either specified coupling law. |
| q-goe-spectral-norm | maximum eigenvalue magnitude | ≤ | $3$ | With probability tending to one as the system size increases. |
| q-edge-gap-growth | $g_{a,n}$ | ≥ | $C_n^{-1}a^{2/3}$ | The random coefficient and index cutoff are tight. |
| q-cap-bottom-scale | $r$ | = | $n^{-1/3+\delta}$ | The buffer exponent is fixed before the system-size limit. |
| q-cap-scale-buffer | $\delta$ | = | $10^{-4}$ | Fixed buffer exponent. |
| q-cap-coordinate-volume | $k_p$ | = | $np^3$ | Observation scale in the prescribed cap range. |
| q-cap-field-normalization | $M_p$ | = | $(np)^{1/2}$ | Observation scale in the prescribed cap range. |
| q-cap-radial-deficit | initial error parameter | = | $0.1$ | Fixed radial covariance deficit on the specified saddle annulus. |
| q-bottom-likelihood-error | expected squared fluctuation of the length parameter | ≤ | $Cn^{-1/4}$ | Smallest observation scale and sufficiently small fixed upper cutoff. |
| q-bottom-mean-error | normalized mean squared error of the message estimate | ≤ | $Cn^{-1/20}$ | Smallest scale after reducing the fixed upper cutoff. |
| q-conditional-variance-energy | total variation of the function | ≤ | $Cp^{-2}\mathcal E(f)+Ce^{-n^\xi}\\|f\\|_\infty^2$ | A common disorder event of arbitrarily high limiting probability; constants independent of the test. |
| q-microscopic-entry-threshold | asymptotic error parameter | ≤ | $n^{-49/100}$ | Bounded interaction operator norm and fixed derivative order before the size limit. |
| q-effective-coefficient-positivity | $c_*$ | > | $0$ | The atom at zero after the finite-size moment limit. |
| q-linear-trace-deterministic-bound | primary auxiliary polynomial | ≤ | $\frac{n^{1/3}}z$ | Positive resolvent parameter and either feature-comparison disorder. |
| q-quadratic-trace-deterministic-bound | secondary auxiliary polynomial | ≤ | $\frac1z$ | Every finite system and positive resolvent parameter. |
| q-pair-energy-average | average edge parameter | ≤ | $\frac2n$ | Uniformly over the base disorder. |
| q-positive-autocorrelation-lower-bound | $\mathcal A_g(s)$ | ≥ | $v(g)\exp\{-s c_*/v(g)\}$ | Positive time and almost every admissible edge configuration. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-spectral-inputs** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-spectral): Apply the eigenvalue-only resolvent estimates and a slow-divergence subsequence criterion. -> Tight edge-growth bounds and renormalized trace control.
- **step-conditioned-edge-law** [claim](#stmt-prop-edge-conditioned-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-edge-conditioned-law): Separate a divergent trace from an absolutely convergent difference and construct consistent conditional densities by Fourier inversion. -> Conditioned edge measure, parameter independence, and spherical marginal convergence.
- **step-bulk-weight** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-bulk-weight) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-bulk-weight): Prove concentration away from the edge and extend using total mass and coordinate moment control. -> Weighted bulk spectral limit.
- **step-haar-bias** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-biased-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-biased-law): Cancel the cube partition denominator under Haar bias and use the uncapped partition comparison. -> Exact spherical identity and negligible frame bias.
- **step-cap-inputs** [claim](#stmt-prop-edge-cap-inputs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-edge-cap-inputs): Import the specified Gaussian cap estimates and retain the radial consequence of the curvature argument. -> Uniform cap geometry, coarse curvature, and projection tails.
- **step-cap-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-static-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-static-interpolation): Control nearby cap weight changes with projection tails, then derive mean comparison from partition-value comparison. -> Static comparison throughout the scale interval.
- **step-path-annulus** [claim](#stmt-lem-observations-annulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-observations-annulus): Concentrate a Gaussian quadratic form after norm conditioning and pass from a scale mesh to the whole observation path. -> Shrinking observation annulus.
- **step-replica-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-observations-replicas) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-observations-replicas): Compare cube and spherical overlap cells and bound angular-integrand oscillation. -> Likelihood second moments and smallest-scale mean comparison.
- **step-cube-marginals** [claim](#stmt-cor-observations-static) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-observations-static): Insert cylinder tests into replica numerators, control their frame variance, and truncate polynomial growth. -> Quenched cube cylinder convergence.
- **step-mean-score** [claim](#stmt-cor-observations-means) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-observations-means): Split likelihood levels, control posterior replica tails, and interpolate mean moments. -> Posterior mean moments and vanishing smallest-scale score.
- **step-score-dissipation** [claim](#stmt-lem-observations-score) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-observations-score): Use the likelihood martingale identity and exponential bracket control. -> Integrated posterior mean-error bound.
- **step-frobenius-error** [claim](#stmt-lem-observations-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-observations-curvature): Apply an integrated Itô identity to the posterior mean difference and stop only the concentration martingale. -> Logarithmically integrated covariance error.
- **step-angular-score** [claim](#stmt-lem-observations-angular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-observations-angular): Average over the unused transverse Haar frame before imposing any refinement-dependent event. -> Angular score decay.
- **step-annular-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-variance-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-variance-extension): Take an infimum of supporting quadratics and compare true and extended transition tails with a frozen Gaussian reference. -> Semiconcave agreement extension with exponentially small transition error.
- **step-radial-removal** [claim](#stmt-lem-variance-radial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-variance-radial): Maximize the error quadratic form over its radial coordinate. -> Transverse positive domination with trace control.
- **step-haar-compression** [claim](#stmt-lem-variance-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-variance-compression): Use spherical concentration and a child-subspace net under the unused conditional Haar rotation. -> Small compressed transverse covariance error.
- **step-positive-curvature** [claim](#stmt-prop-variance-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-variance-compression-2): Average parent transitions and iterate a contracting scale inequality using integrated Frobenius control. -> Scale-integrable positive covariance excess.
- **step-fixed-scale-start** [claim](#stmt-lem-variance-start) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-variance-start): Apply the fixed-duration entropy continuation to bounded-density perturbations and tensorize variance at the product endpoint. -> Uniform fixed-scale variance-energy bound.
- **step-exact-variance** [claim](#stmt-prop-variance-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-variance-start-2): Integrate the posterior variance identity backward with logarithmically integrable covariance errors. -> Conditional variance bound without a power loss.
- **step-compensated-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-gradient-compensated) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gradient-compensated): Damp Hermite grades, identify symmetric arrays with a common one-place trace, and realize their cores as square-sum polynomials. -> Polynomial approximation in the compensated graph norm.
- **step-gradient-domain** [claim](#stmt-prop-gradient-domain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gradient-compensated-2): Prove angular uniqueness and closability, localize a polar product chart, apply compensated density, and identify the tail-trivial kernel. -> Exact closed conditioned-gradient domain and constant kernel.
- **step-observation-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-lem-gradient-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gradient-smoothing): Use posterior covariance and variance bounds, then expand Gaussian translations in Hermite tensors. -> Bounded slopes and controlled smoothing errors.
- **step-gradient-identification** [claim](#stmt-prop-gradient-observation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gradient-observation): Transport angular slope pairings to the smallest scale and pass its integration-by-parts identity to the limit. -> Weak slope limits equal the conditioned gradient.
- **step-connected-contractions** [claim](#stmt-lem-micro-contractions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-micro-contractions): Expand fixed-order field differences into connected graphs and count conditional orthogonal Haar pairings. -> Deterministic factored microscopic contractions and vanishing unmarked cap projections.
- **step-weighted-recursion** [claim](#stmt-lem-micro-weighted-recursion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-micro-weighted-recursion): Separate differentiated conditional means, retain signed operator estimates, and control the generator in the weighted tilted measure. -> Fixed-order weighted difference recursion.
- **step-moment-density** [claim](#stmt-cor-micro-moment-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-micro-moment-density): Bound moments by the difference recursion and use analytic Fourier transforms of the symmetric square-root lift. -> Unique positive moment limit and weighted polynomial density.
- **step-microscopic-measure** [claim](#stmt-prop-micro-moment-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-micro-moment-density-2): Combine fixed-order contraction limits with moment determinacy and transfer biased calculations to quenched integrals. -> Deterministic microscopic energy measure and corrector slope identities.
- **step-cylinder-recovery** [claim](#stmt-lem-micro-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-micro-recovery): Add a generator-polynomial microscopic correction, bound its value norm, and identify its slope energy by contraction limits. -> Strong cylinder recovery with prescribed polynomial energy.
- **step-positive-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-lem-micro-positivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-micro-positivity): Apply the coarse gradient bound to fixed-polynomial recovery sequences and then approximate the zero atom. -> Strict positivity of the effective coefficient.
- **step-multiplied-divergence** [claim](#stmt-lem-micro-divergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-micro-divergence): Use conditional single-site integration and cylinder Lipschitz bounds to remove the multiplication error. -> Vanishing multiplied-divergence remainder.
- **step-relaxed-energy** [claim](#stmt-thm-micro-relaxation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-micro-relaxation): Test arbitrary slopes against atom-isolating correctors, reconcile the successive limits, and optimize the dual energy inequality. -> Matching sharp lower and recovery energy bounds.
- **step-fixed-resolvents** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-fixed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-fixed): Apply lower energy and recovery inequalities to the resolvent variational principle. -> Joint fixed-coordinate resolvent convergence.
- **step-vector-replacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-replacement): Sum posterior martingale pairing increments down a geometric scale list using vector variance and mean-moment bounds. -> Uniform replacement of tail pairings by smallest-cap pairings.
- **step-gaussian-trace-tail** [claim](#stmt-prop-trace-goe) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-replacement-2): Use angular vector fields with summable squared inverse gaps, control their inverse-radius moments, and test the normalized tail optimizer. -> Vanishing trace tail, finite limiting series, and joint full Gaussian trace convergence.
- **step-quadratic-trace** [claim](#stmt-prop-trace-second) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-trace-second): Smooth the vector test and bound the conditional quadratic feature by posterior replica overlaps. -> Vanishing Gaussian quadratic-feature trace.
- **step-mixture-overlap** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-overlap): Combine annealed replica entropy gaps with a quenched partition lower bound and the weighted-overlap identity. -> Uniform overlap suppression and small weighted pair correlations.
- **step-residual-sensitivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-residual): Compute endpoint energy multipliers and express the first form residual through conditional endpoint gradients. -> Pointwise and averaged pair residual bounds.
- **step-average-sensitivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-average): Reweight the static overlap estimate by the optimizer mass-plus-energy density and combine it with endpoint counting. -> Small expected pair sensitivities for the comparison features.
- **step-fourth-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-derivative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-derivative): Expand the analytic perturbed inverse form around its optimizer and differentiate the compactified trace. -> Fourth-derivative replacement bound.
- **step-trace-universality** [claim](#stmt-thm-comparison-universality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-derivative-2): Use independent completion to bound Taylor remainders under the iid mixture, transfer quadratic traces first, then spin traces. -> Common finite-dimensional resolvent trace law under Gaussian and sign disorder.
- **step-spectral-continuity** [claim](#stmt-lem-functional-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-functional-spectral): Compactify the weighted spectral measures, identify their laws by a determining resolvent algebra, and apply the continuous Laplace map. -> Functional Laplace-transform convergence at positive times.
- **step-functional-identification** [claim](#stmt-prop-functional-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-functional-identification): Construct the measurable limiting spectral measure from countable-core resolvent pairings and apply spectral continuity. -> Common Borel functional limit.
- **step-edge-shifts** [claim](#stmt-lem-functional-shift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-functional-shift): Localize ordered finite-dimensional GOE density ratios and cancel divergent Gaussian and Vandermonde contributions. -> Absolute continuity under finite order-preserving edge shifts.
- **step-limit-randomness** [claim](#stmt-prop-functional-randomness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-functional-randomness): Lower the leading edge point, compare the conditioned laws by exponential tilt, and use the coordinate spectral lower bound. -> Essentially unbounded positive-time autocorrelation.
- **step-main-functional-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-functional-randomness-2): Combine functional identification, the constant form kernel, coordinate sign symmetry, spectral monotonicity, and limit randomness. -> Functional universality with positive values, almost-sure decay, and surviving disorder randomness.
- **step-closed-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-support-constant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-support-constant): Modify finitely many variances to concentrate the leading coordinate near the sign-separated regions, build a low-energy distinguishing function, and bound the remaining trace by angular integration. -> The constant function belongs to the closed local support.

          "exact_quote": "Under $\\mathbf P$, $U^{\\mathsf T}X$ has exactly the spherical Gibbs law in spectral coordinates. Moreover, on the eigenvalue events used below, $$\\label{eq:edge-uncapped-comparison}\n E_U(L_\\circ-1)^2=o(1).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-227.2"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-edge-cap-inputs",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite field radius $R$ and positive fixed error tolerances. There are sufficiently small constants $p_{\\rm top},s_0>0$ such that the following hold with probability tending to one in the range $np^3\\ge c\\sqrt{\\log n}$ and $p\\le p_{\\rm top}$.*\n\n**Spectral and spherical estimates, at every scale.* The spectral dimension satisfies $\\dim\\mathcal H_p\\asymp k_p$, $d_1\\ge-o(p^2)$ uniformly in the cap range, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}R_{p,0}^2=O(n/p)$. On $|\\alpha|\\le s_0p^2$, $\\ell_p(\\alpha)\\asymp p^2M_p$ and $\\ell_p'(\\alpha)\\asymp M_p$. For $\\|h\\|\\le Rp^2M_p$, the saddle precision has gap comparable to $p^2$. At $\\alpha=\\alpha_p(h)$, the density at $n$ of the squared norm of $\\mathsf N(R_{p,\\alpha}h,R_{p,\\alpha})$ is comparable to $\\sqrt{p/n}$. After multiplication by $e^{v^{\\mathsf T}X}$ and renormalization, the Gaussian mean becomes $R_{p,\\alpha}(h+v)$ while its covariance remains $R_{p,\\alpha}$; its squared-norm density at $n$ is at most a fixed multiple of $\\sqrt{p/n}$. For $m$ replicas, where $m$ is fixed, consider rowwise linear or quadratic tilts that preserve Gaussianity. The joint density of their squared norms is at most $C_m(p/n)^{m/2}$ under the following hypotheses. The spectral rows remain independent after the rowwise tilt, the tilted precision is positive definite, and on at least $ck_p$ rows the post-tilt row covariance $C_a$ satisfies $c p^{-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}_m\\preceq C_a\\preceq C p^{-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}_m$. All comparison constants here are fixed.*\n\n\n**Curvature estimates, at every scale.* On the annulus, for any prescribed fixed $\\epsilon_0>0$, $$\\label{eq:source-6}\n K_p\\preceq(1+\\epsilon_0)p^{-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}_{\\mathcal H_p},\\qquad\n p^2(K_p)_{ee}\\le1-\\epsilon_1,\n \\quad e=h/\\|h\\|,\\quad \\epsilon_1=0.1.$$ On the entire field ball one also has $K_p\\preceq C_Rp^{-2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}_{\\mathcal H_p}$, where $C_R$ may be chosen before $\\epsilon_0$.*\n\n**Static comparisons and cube projection tails, on geometric lists.* For any prescribed fixed geometric list of scales, uniformly over the field ball, $$|\\phi_p(h)-\\phi_p^{\\rm sp}(h)|\\le\\epsilon_v k_p,\n \\qquad\n \\|P_pm_p(h)-R_{p,\\alpha_p(h)}h\\|\\le\\epsilon_gM_p.$$ Any fixed finite family of such lists is allowed. For each $h\\in\\mathcal H_p$ with $\\|h\\|\\le Rp^2M_p$, the cube posterior $\\mu_{p,h}$ satisfies, uniformly for every $v\\ge v_0$ with a sufficiently large fixed threshold $v_0$, $$\\mu_{p,h}\\{\\|P_pX\\|>vM_p\\}\\le C\\exp(-ak_pv^6).$$ For $v>p^{-1/2}$ the event is empty, since $\\|X\\|=\\sqrt n$. Here $a>0$, and the same bound holds under the uncapped zero-field cube measure $\\mu$ at the same list scales.*\n\n*The spherical projection-tail estimate holds at every scale in the stated range, for capped bounded-field and uncapped zero-field measures. Also, at every such cap scale, for independent spherical replicas at a common field in the prescribed ball, $$(\\mu_{p,h}^{\\rm sp})^{\\otimes2}\n \\{|n^{-1}X^{\\mathsf T}X'|>\\rho\\}\n \\le C\\exp(-cn\\rho^3),\\qquad Cp\\le\\rho\\le\\rho_0,$$ for a sufficiently small fixed $\\rho_0>0$. A fixed finite number of replicas obeys the corresponding bound for any pair.",
          "source_id": "PM-227.2"
        }

          "exact_quote": "For $r\\le p\\le p_*$ in the range of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:variance-main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:variance-main\">16</a>, $$Q_\\mu\\|\\nabla_{u_p}F_p\\|^2\n       \\le C T\\mathcal E(f)+o(1),\n \\label{eq:source-26}$$ and $$\\begin{split}\n \\|n^{1/3}(f-S_pf)\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}\n       &\\le Cp^{-1}\\sqrt{T\\mathcal E(f)}+o(1),\\\\\n \\left\\|n^{1/3}(D_iS_pf)_i-J_p^f\\right\\|_{L^2(\\mu;\\mathbb R^n)}\n       &\\le Cp\\sqrt{T\\mathcal E(f)}+o(1).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:source-27}$$ In particular, $J_p^f$ is bounded in $L^2(\\mu;\\mathbb R^n)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-227.2"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gradient-observation",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $f_n$ satisfies the preceding bounds and has weak cylinder limit $F$. Choose a subsequence on which $T\\mathcal E(f_n)$ tends to its liminf. After further extraction in the quenched convention, there is a sequence $q_j\\downarrow0$ such that the spectral components of $\\overline{J^f}_{q_j}$ have weak cylinder limits $G^{(j)}$ as $n\\to\\infty$. Every weak cluster point of $(G^{(j)})$ in $L^2(\\Pi;\\ell^2)$ equals $\\nabla F$. In particular, $F$ belongs to the form domain of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:gradient-domain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:gradient-domain\">22</a> and $$\\|\\nabla F\\|_{L^2(\\Pi;\\ell^2)}\n \\le C\\left(\\liminf_{n\\to\\infty}T\\mathcal E(f_n)\\right)^{1/2}.\n \\label{eq:source-28}$$ The constant is the same for all sequences with the stated size bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-227.2"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-gradient-observation"
    },
